or, to maintain in a particular fashion or manner."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"She wore a smile all day.","type":"example"},{"text":"He walked out of the courtroom wearing an air of satisfaction.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To have on:","To bear or display in one's aspect or appearance."],"tags":["transitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"You're going to wear a hole in the bottom of those shoes.","type":"example"},{"text":"The water has slowly worn a channel into these rocks.","type":"example"},{"text":"Long illness had worn the bloom from her cheeks.","type":"example"},{"text":"Exile had worn the man to a shadow.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To erode:","To eat away at, erode, diminish, or consume gradually; to cause a gradual deterioration in; to produce (some change) through attrition, exposure, or constant use."]},{"examples":[{"text":"The tiles were wearing thin due to years of children's feet.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To erode:","To undergo gradual deterioration; become impaired; be reduced or consumed gradually due to any continued process, activity, or use."],"tags":["copulative","intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"His neverending criticism has finally worn my patience.","type":"example"},{"text":"Toil and care soon wear the spirit.","type":"example"},{"text":"Our physical advantage allowed us to wear the other team out and win.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To erode:","To exhaust, fatigue, expend, or weary."]},{"examples":[{"text":"Her high pitched voice is really wearing on me lately.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To erode:","(in the phrase \"wearing on (someone)\") To cause annoyance, irritation, fatigue, or weariness near the point of an exhaustion of patience."],"tags":["colloquial","intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"I know you don't like working with him, but you'll just have to wear it.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To endure:","To overcome one's reluctance and endure a (previously specified) situation."],"raw_tags":["with \"it\""],"tags":["colloquial"]},{"examples":[{"text":"Don't worry, this fabric will wear. These pants will last you for years.","type":"example"},{"text":"This color wears so well. I must have washed this sweater a thousand times.","type":"example"},{"text":"I have to say, our friendship has worn pretty well.","type":"example"},{"text":"It's hard to get to know him, but he wears well.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To endure:","To last or remain durable under hard use or over time; to retain usefulness, value, or desirable qualities under any continued strain or long period of time; sometimes said of a person, regarding the quality of being easy or difficult to tolerate."],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"examples":[{"text":"wear on, wear away.","type":"example"},{"text":"As the years wore on, we seemed to have less and less in common.","type":"example"}],"glosses":["To endure:","To pass slowly, gradually or tediously."],"raw_tags":["of time"],"tags":["intransitive"]},{"glosses":["To bring (a sailing vessel) onto the other tack by bringing the wind around the stern (as opposed to tacking when the wind is brought around the bow); to come round on another tack by turning away from the wind."],"synonyms":[{"word":"gybe"}],"topics":["nautical","transport"]}],"sounds":[{"enpr":"wâr"},{"ipa":"/wɛə/","tags":["Received-Pronunciation"]},{"ipa":"/wɛː/","tags":["British","Southern","Standard"]},{"ipa":"/wɛ(ə)ɹ/","tags":["General-American"]},{"ipa":"[wɛɚ]","tags":["General-American"]},{"ipa":"[wɛɹ]","tags":["General-American"]},{"ipa":"/weː/","tags":["Australia"]},{"ipa":"/weə/","tags":["New-Zealand"]},{"enpr":"wîr"},{"ipa":"/wiə/"},{"ipa":"/weɾ/","tags":["Scotland"]},{"enpr":"wûr"},{"ipa":"/wɜː(ɹ)/"},{"enpr":"wîr"},{"ipa":"/wɪja(r)/"}],"upstream_line":35324,"upstream_offset":244337255,"upstream_record_sha256":"bdcce3aa4cc074b1479354722860d6969a79c60bf5bda519219f376e6fe01604","word":"wear"}
